    if (markedRef.current) deferredPick.cancel();

  // 7Sage sets both the stimulus and the answer choices at 19px and the

  // couple of pixels apart. The stimulus carries the extra weight of the serif
  // face; stem and choices stay on the sans stack at the same size.
  const baseSize = 19 * fontScale;
  const officialContentTextStyle: React.CSSProperties = {
    color: "#111827",
    fontFamily: '"Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif',
    fontSize: `${baseSize}px`,
    fontWeight: 400,
    lineHeight: 1.6,
  };
  const stimStyle: React.CSSProperties = {
    ...officialContentTextStyle,
    fontFamily: "Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif",
    fontSize: `${19 * fontScale}px`,
    lineHeight: 1.7,
    // Fraunces is a variable font with an optical-size axis. Left on `auto` the
    // browser picks the cut from the rendered px size, which drifts as the
    // student changes fontScale. Pin it to the text-optimised end so hairlines
    // stay the same weight at every scale.
    fontOpticalSizing: "none",
    fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
  };
  const stemStyle: React.CSSProperties = officialContentTextStyle;
  // The answer-choice text and letter badges used Tailwind's `text-sm`/`text-xs`,
  // which are hard px values and silently overrode the inherited scaled size —
  // so fontScale grew the stimulus and stem while the answers stayed at 14px.
  const choiceTextStyle: React.CSSProperties = { fontSize: `${baseSize}px` };
  const choiceBadgeStyle: React.CSSProperties = { fontSize: `${baseSize * 0.78}px` };

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero {
  background-color: var(--observatory-surface-image);
  background-image: url("/mainhub/hub-horizon.png");
  /* WebP is 37KB against the PNG's 384KB; the PNG line above stays as the
     fallback for anything that cannot parse image-set. */
  background-image: image-set(
    url("/mainhub/hub-horizon.webp") type("image/webp"),
    url("/mainhub/hub-horizon.png") type("image/png")
  );
  background-position: center 68%;
  background-repeat: no-repeat;
  background-size: cover;
  border: 0;
  border-radius: var(--observatory-radius-shell);
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 1px var(--observatory-ring),
    var(--observatory-shadow-image);
  color: var(--observatory-ink);
  min-height: 0;
  overflow: hidden;
  position: relative;
}

/* Scrim. Mandatory, not decorative: the greeting sits over the image's bright
   terminator at some crops and is unreadable without it.

   The middle stop was 0.35, and that was measured by eye. Sampling the actual
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero-grid {
  align-items: center;
  display: grid;
  gap: 28px;
  grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) 392px;
  min-height: 280px;
  padding: var(--observatory-space-4) var(--observatory-space-6) var(--observatory-space-5);
  position: relative;
  z-index: 2;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-greeting {
  align-self: end;
  gap: var(--observatory-space-5);
  max-width: 440px;
  min-height: 0;
  padding: 0 0 6px;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-smart {
  align-self: center;
  max-width: 440px;
  min-height: 0;
  padding: 0 0 6px;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-smart h1 {
  color: var(--observatory-ink);
  font-family: "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;
  font-size: clamp(1.35rem, 3cqi, 2rem);
  font-weight: 600;
  letter-spacing: -0.025em;
  line-height: 1.2;
  text-wrap: balance;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-smart p {
  text-shadow: 0 1px 2px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.3);
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-greeting h1 {
  color: var(--observatory-ink);
  font-family: var(--observatory-display-font);
  font-size: clamp(1.9rem, 3.4vw, var(--observatory-text-display));
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 60;
  font-weight: 400;
  letter-spacing: var(--observatory-track-display);
  line-height: 1.08;
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-console {
  align-self: center;
  backdrop-filter: blur(12px);
  -webkit-backdrop-filter: blur(12px);
  background: var(--observatory-glass);
  border: 0;
  border-radius: var(--observatory-radius-panel);
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 1px var(--observatory-ring-glass),
    var(--observatory-shadow-glass);
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: var(--observatory-space-3);
  margin: 0;
  max-width: none;
  padding: var(--observatory-space-5);
  position: relative;
  z-index: 3;
}

## assistant

I've started a sub-agent on the facts: every page and question player in the app, where the question text is stored, and how long the longest questions run. The questions below don't depend on those answers. The ones that do will come next round.

❓ **Q1** - **Which screen size is the target?** "Fits in one glance" only means something against a specific window size. Your screenshots are from a 1920×1080 screen at 100% zoom. After the taskbar and browser bars, that leaves a window about 1900×930. Students also use 13" MacBooks (about 1440×800 after browser bars) and cheaper 1366×768 laptops (about 1366×650).
- (a) Your screen only: 1920×930
- (b) MacBook floor: 1440×800
- (c) Cheap-laptop floor: 1366×650

➡️ (b), 1440×800 as the smallest screen that must fit with no scrolling, checked on your 1920×930 as well. Squeezing into 1366×650 would force the text too small for everyone else.

---

❓ **Q2** - **What if a question still doesn't fit?** Some LR questions are just long: a two-speaker stimulus plus five long choices. Options:
- (a) Auto-fit: text shrinks step by step until the question fits, down to a floor (say 15px). Past that floor, only the column that's too long scrolls.
- (b) One fixed size for every question, and the column scrolls when it runs over.
- (c) A different layout for long questions, such as narrower margins or the stem moved over to the stimulus side.

➡️ (a). Every question fits whenever it physically can, and nothing ever drops below a readable floor. The student's own font-size setting stays on top of it.

---

❓ **Q3** - **Can we go below 7Sage's 19px?** The code pins text at 19px so it matches 7Sage side by side. Fitting every question means going below that on smaller screens.

➡️ Yes. Keep 19px wherever it fits, and let fit win over matching 7Sage. A student reading 17px they can see all of beats 19px they have to scroll.

---

❓ **Q4** - **Which pages are in scope?** Two groups:
- **Question screens:** LR drill, RC, blind review, section review, Testing Centre sections
- **Hub pages:** Home, Drill, Analytics, Assigned work, Review, History, and the rest of the sidebar

➡️ Every student page in the Classroom, done in order: question screens first (that's the core complaint), then Drill hub and Home, then everything else. RC passages are an exception. A full passage can't fit at a readable size, so there the rule is that the passage scrolls on its own while the question and choices stay fully visible.

---

❓ **Q5** - **What does "fits" mean on a hub page?** A question screen should fit completely. Hub pages like Home and Drill have more content than one screen can hold. Options:
- (a) Everything on the page fits with no scrolling at all.
- (b) The important part shows on first load without scrolling (on Drill: Smart Drill, Build a drill, Resume, and Assigned work), and lower sections scroll.
- (c) Only spacing is tightened, with no fold rule.

➡️ (b). Each hub gets a named set of things that must show on first load, and I'll propose that set page by page next round.

---

❓ **Q6** - **Phones and tablets?**

➡️ Out of scope for this pass. The target is laptop and desktop. Phone layout is a separate project.

---

❓ **Q7** - **What happens to [#135](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/135)?** It's the quick fix from earlier and hasn't been checked in a browser.

➡️ Don't merge it on its own. Keep the branch as the starting point and replace its fixed step-down with the auto-fit from Q2, so we ship one tested change instead of two.

**1. Student routes (`src/App.tsx`)**

*Question players:* none of these sit inside ClassroomLayout; each one renders full-screen inside `OfficialDrillFrame`.
- LR drill, `/drill`: `src/pages/Drill.tsx` (5,140 lines).
- RC player, `/rc/drill`: `src/features/rc/pages/RcPlayer.tsx`. `/rc/results/:sessionId` and `/rc/search` are also in `src/features/rc/pages/`.
- Blind review happens inside Drill. The live one is `src/components/drill/EnhancedBlindReview.tsx`. An older `BlindReviewFlow.tsx` is still mounted at Drill.tsx:3367 and uses a single-column Card with `max-w-4xl`, not the split layout.
- Section review, inside Drill: `src/components/drill/SectionReviewWalk.tsx`. Results screens are `LRSectionResults.tsx` and `PracticeSetResults.tsx`.
- Testing centre, `/testing`: `src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx`. A PT run at `/testing/test/:runId` (`src/pages/PracticeTestRun.tsx`) sends each LR or RC section to `/drill` or `/rc/drill` through `lrLegLaunch` and `rcLegHref`, so PTs use the same two players.
- Also: `/section/:assignmentId` (`SectionDirections.tsx`), `/guided/:id`, `/guided-drills/:slug`, `/activities/*` (ActivityRunner).

*Hubs:* all inside `ClassroomLayout` (`src/components/classroom/shell/ClassroomLayout.tsx`).
- `/classroom` → `pages/Classroom.tsx`, and `/classroom/drill` → `AcademyMainhub.tsx`.
- Other `/classroom/` pages: `uploads`, `account`, `recaps`, `admissions`, `item/:id`, `ta/:id`, `:assignmentId`.
- Top-level pages in the same shell: `/testing`, `/flagged`, `/journal`, `/inbox`, `/sets`, `/activities`, `/analytics` (`Analytics.tsx`), `/history` (`DrillHistory.tsx`), `/schedule`, `/booking`, `/bootcamps`.

**2. How the players render questions**
- **LR, blind review and section review** all use `src/components/drill/official/OfficialQuestionPane.tsx` on top of `DrillSplit.tsx`. Columns are split 53fr / 47fr from `lg` up and stack below that.
  - Text size is `calc(fontScale * clamp(17px, 2.2vh, 19px))` for stimulus, stem and choices. Answer letters are 0.78× that.
  - Line height is 1.6 for the stimulus (Fraunces, max width `52ch`) and 1.5 for stem and choices (Helvetica Neue).
  - Stimulus column padding is `px-6 pt-5 pb-6 sm:pl-9 sm:pr-8`. Question column is `px-6 py-5 sm:pl-10 sm:pr-8`. Each choice is `px-3 py-2` with `space-y-1.5`.
  - `fontScale` comes from `officialFontScale` in Drill.tsx:547 and defaults to 1.
- **Scrolling differs between the two players:**
  - LR does not scroll its columns independently. Drill.tsx never passes `fillViewport`, so the frame gets `min-h-screen` and `min-h-[700px]`, and the whole page scrolls.
  - RC does scroll each column on its own. RcPlayer.tsx:651 passes `fillViewport`, which gives the frame `h-screen` and a full-height card.
- **RC** renders through `RcExamPane.tsx`, `RcPassagePane.tsx`, `RcQuestionPane.tsx` and `RcChoiceRow.tsx`, on the same `DrillSplit` 53/47 layout.
  - Sizes are fixed px, not tied to screen height: passage `17 * fontScale` px at line height 1.7 plus extra leading; question and choices `16 * fontScale` px at 1.6.
  - Padding is `px-6 pb-7 pt-6 sm:pl-9` for the passage and `px-6 py-7 sm:pl-10` for the question.

**3. Where the LR question text lives**
- It is in the repo, not loaded from the database: 156 files named `public/data/PT###-S#-LR.json`, merged into `_bank.generated.json`. Each question has `stimulus`, `questionStem` and `answerChoices`. It is loaded by `src/lib/questionLoader.ts`.
- A `public.lr_questions` table exists in migrations, but the player reads the JSON. RC works the same way, from `public/data/rc/RC-PT###.json`.
- Character lengths across 3,965 questions:

| | Median | 90th percentile | 99th percentile | Max |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Stimulus + stem + choices | 1,041 | 1,391 | 1,861 | 2,412 |
| Stimulus | 388 | 547 | 716 | 868 |
| All five choices | 542 | 856 | 1,405 | 1,923 |
| Longest single choice | 137 | 211 | 327 | 463 |

- Longest questions overall: PT114-S2-Q23 (2,412), PT113-S2-Q21 and PT119-S2-Q21 (2,299 each), PT134-S3-Q18, PT130-S4-Q25, PT108-S3-Q24.
- Longest stimuli: PT118-S3-Q7/Q8 (868) and PT110-S3-Q7/Q8 (825).

**4. Existing global layout controls**
- There is no `html` font-size rule and no zoom setting. The only base size is on `body` in `src/index.css:204`: 14px, rising to 16px at 640px and wider.
- The player card is capped at `max-w-[1400px]` in `OfficialDrillFrame.tsx:141`, on a `#1A1635` background. The optional notes panel adds a `w-[380px]` column beside it.
- Classroom shell CSS is in `src/components/classroom/classroom.css`:
  - The sidebar is 336px wide and `100dvh` tall, sticky.

  - The shell uses `min-height: 100dvh` throughout.
  - The section list switches to an overlay below 1280px (below 1400px for the journal).</result>

The research sub-agent has finished. Two findings bear on your answers to Q1 and Q2:

- **How big the fitting job is:** the text of all 3,965 LR questions lives in the app's files, so I can measure every one of them and don't need to sample. A typical question runs about 1,040 characters. Nine in ten are under 1,390. The longest is PT114-S2-Q23 at 2,412, more than twice the typical one. That spread is why a single fixed text size can't fit them all, which is the case for auto-fit in Q2.
- **LR and RC scroll differently today:** the LR screen scrolls as one whole page, and it has a minimum height of 700px. RC already scrolls each column on its own. Whatever we pick in Q2, LR will need to be switched to the RC behavior.

I'm waiting on your answers to Q1–Q7 before the next round.

## assistant

I looked at both screenshots and measured them. Your Chrome zoom is at 100%, as you said. The page looks zoomed in anyway because **Windows itself is set to 125% display scaling**. I worked this out from the pixels rather than by checking your settings. The question text is set to 19px in the code, and it measures about 24 screen pixels tall. The line spacing is also exactly 1.25 times what the code specifies. 125% is the setting Windows recommends on most 1080p laptops, so most of your students on Windows will see exactly what you see.

That changes the target. Your screen is 1920 pixels wide, but the page only has room for about **1536×690** of its own units once the browser bars are counted. That's smaller than the 1440×800 MacBook case I proposed. It explains why only three choices fit, and why the Drill hub banner and sidebar look oversized.

You're also right that every question needs different spacing. The longest LR question is more than twice the length of a typical one, so a single fixed text size can't fit all of them. That's a yes to auto-fit.

Also, the live site is still running the old code. Nothing from [#135](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/135) has shipped, so these screenshots are the "before".

❓ **Q1** - **Target screen (revised)**: default settings everywhere: Chrome at 100% and Windows at its recommended scaling. The target is then the page area of a 1080p laptop at 125%, about 1536×690. At Windows 150% (common on 13–14" laptops) it drops to about 1280×580, which is very tight.

➡️ Every question must fit at **1536×690**. At 1280×580, the typical question should fit, and the very long ones may scroll within their own column. Both get checked, along with a MacBook.

---

❓ **Q2** - **Auto-fit (confirming)**: every question measures itself when it opens. If it doesn't fit, the text shrinks one step at a time down to a floor. Past the floor, only the long column scrolls. The student's Aa font-size control still works on top of this.

➡️ Yes. Floor at 15px.

---

❓ **Q3** - **"I don't like this. Okay. I like it."** I can't tell what this refers to. Which of these do you like, and which don't you?
- (a) The **look** of the question screen (fonts, the boxed selected choice, overall design)
- (b) The **fit** (big text, choices cut off)

➡️ My read: you like the look and dislike the fit, so I keep the design and change only size and spacing. Correct me if you meant something else.

---

❓ **Q4** - **What shrinks first**: the question screen has fixed parts too, not just text. The "Welcome, Joshua" line and breadcrumb take about 100px, the toolbar about 65px, and there's padding around the white card. On a 690-tall page, that's a quarter of the height gone before any text.

➡️ Tighten the fixed parts first: put the breadcrumb, Answer, Explanation and Pause on one thin row, and cut the dark margin around the card. Shrink text only after that.

---

❓ **Q5** - **Pages in scope and order**: every student Classroom page. Question screens (LR, RC, blind review, section review, Testing Centre) come first, then Drill hub and Home, then the rest. RC passages scroll on their own; the question and choices always fit.

➡️ Yes, in that order.

---

❓ **Q6** - **Hub pages**: the important part shows on first load without scrolling, and lower sections scroll. On Drill that's Smart Drill, Build a drill, Resume and Assigned work. The sidebar also shrinks: it's 336px wide today, and at 125% that takes about a fifth of the screen width.

➡️ Yes, and bring the sidebar down to about 240px.

---

❓ **Q7** - **Phones** stay out of this pass, and **#135** stays unmerged and becomes the starting branch for this work.

➡️ Both yes.

## assistant

I opened the Drill page and Home in your Chrome and measured them. You're right that Chrome is at 100%. Windows has its own, separate size setting, and on your laptop that one is at **125%**.

**What Chrome reported**
- Pixel ratio 1.25: Windows is enlarging everything by 125%.
- Screen reported as 1536×864, not 1920×1080.
- Space the page actually gets: **1536×695**.

**Why both are true**
- Chrome's zoom (the 100% in your menu) only enlarges web pages.
- Windows enlarges everything on the screen: menus, taskbar, and web pages.
- To see it: Windows Settings, then System, then Display, then Scale. It will say 125% with "(Recommended)" next to it.
- Windows picks that setting on its own for most 1080p laptops. So 125% is the normal setup for your students too. We design for it, not against it.

**What doesn't fit at 1536×695**

*Drill page* (the whole page is 1001 tall, so about 30% sits below the first screen):
- Sidebar: 336 wide, which is 22% of the width.
- PrepTest / Section / Question row: 88 tall.
- Smart Drill banner: 280 tall.
- Your practice and Assigned work start at 432, so only the top 263 of them shows.

*Home*:
- The greeting card is 271 tall, and "Joshua Fisseha" wraps onto a second line at 114 tall.
- Below it, the message card is 145 tall and Next seven days is 123 tall.
- The first screen holds those three and nothing else.

❓ **Q1** - **Target size**: which screen do we design for first?

➡️ Your setup, 1536×695, is the main target, since it's what most Windows laptops show by default. I'd check two more: 1920×950 (a big monitor) so it doesn't look sparse, and 1280×580 (Windows at 150%) so nothing breaks.

---

❓ **Q2** - **Drill page, first screen**: what has to be visible without scrolling?

➡️ At minimum: the PrepTest/Question row, Smart Drill, Build a drill, Resume your drill, Top priority, and the first two Assigned work items. To get there:
- Shrink the PrepTest row from 88 to about 56.
- Shrink the banner from 280 to about 170.
- Your practice and Assigned work then start around 250 instead of 432.

---

❓ **Q3** - **Sidebar**: 336 wide with roomy rows.

➡️ Bring it to about 240 wide with tighter rows. That gives every Classroom page back about 100 pixels of width, and the whole menu fits without its own scrollbar.

---

❓ **Q4** - **Home, first screen**: what has to be visible without scrolling?

➡️ Greeting, latest message, Next seven days, and the top of Today/assigned work. To get there:
- Shrink the greeting card from 271 to about 170.
- Keep your name on one line.
- Tighten the message card.

---

❓ **Q5** - **How far the tightening goes**: this decides whether a big monitor keeps today's spacious look.
- (a) Tighten everything, for every screen size.
- (b) Tighten only when the screen is short. Big monitors keep today's look, and laptops get the compact version automatically.

➡️ (b). It fixes your laptop without making a large monitor look cramped.

---

❓ **Q6** - **Order of work**: which page first?

➡️ Drill page first, then Home, then the sidebar, since it's shared by every page. The question screen comes after, as you said.

CONTEXT-MAP.md
5:## Terms
7:**Classroom frame** — the persistent layout every Classroom route is drawn in: the cream left rail, an optional middle list, and the white reading surface. Implemented by `ClassroomLayout`. Navigating between surfaces it draws swaps the content only and never remounts the rail. A page "sits in the Classroom frame" regardless of its URL; `/flagged` and `/journal` both do.
9:**StudentShell** — the older, separate frame with its own 240px rail, still drawing `/mainhub`, `/practice` and the rest. A student moving between a Classroom-frame page and a StudentShell page sees the whole page rebuild. Reducing that boundary is ongoing work, one surface at a time.
11:**Reading column** — the white content column. Most routes use one reading column on the right. A Wrong answers detail uses a four-column Classroom frame: cream rail, entries list, vertical question, and review panel. Below its journal-only breakpoints the entries list becomes an overlay and the review panel moves under the question.
13:**Drill landing workspace** — the pre-question surface at `/classroom/drill`, drawn inside the Classroom frame. It includes drill selection, launch controls, practice insights, and today's work; it excludes the official question-answering experience.
16:**Question finder** — the one control that turns something a student can say about a question into that question opened. It takes free text, matched against question text, and it takes a question address (PT 90, Section 1, Question 12) typed or picked from PrepTest / Section / Question selectors. It searches both banks and groups its results by bank, Logical Reasoning first, because a hit lands differently in each: an LR hit opens that one question, an RC hit opens its whole passage with every question on it. The finder is a finding aid, not the scope of the work it opens. It appears on Classroom Home and in the Drill landing workspace; it never builds a set, it hands that to the Drill console.
19:**Drill console** — the control surface inside the Drill landing workspace where a student assembles a set: the filters, the preview of the exact set, and the start action. Finding one named question is not its job; that is the Question finder.
22:**Drill canvas** — the route-specific visual composition to the right of the persistent Classroom rail, containing the Drill hero, Drill console, practice/insight workspace, and Today focus. The Classroom rail is not part of the canvas.
25:**Section** — a middle-column list paired with the reading column: Notes and recaps, Assigned work, Flagged. A section is picked in the rail, lists its items in the middle column, and shows the chosen item in the reading column.
27:**Flag** — a come-back-to-this marker the student raises *during* a question, from a drill or a blind review. It records intent to return, and says nothing about whether the question was answered correctly. Stored in `flagged_questions`.
29:**Flagged question** — a question carrying a flag. The Flagged section is the student's unfinished business: questions they chose to set aside. Clearing a flag is the student's own act (Unflag), not something the system does when they get the question right.
31:**Wrong answer** — a question the student actually missed, recorded in `wrong_answer_journal` and worked through in Wrong answers at `/journal`. Distinct from a flag in both meaning and storage: a flag is chosen, a wrong answer is earned. A question can be both, one, or neither. Do not treat the two lists as views of the same thing.
33:**Journal entry** — the review of one wrong answer at `/journal/:qid`. The URL is the selection: it resolves only by `qid`, never by the journal row id, and `/journal` does not silently select the first entry. Its review fields autosave without a visible save state.
35:**Ask** — sending a question to the coach from the surface the student is looking at, with their own comment attached. Always labelled "Ask", never with the coach's name; th...

**Activity**:
One bespoke skill exercise — a titled, self-contained set of authored items with an answer key, worked through with a coach (e.g. "Negation Station"). The unit a teacher assigns.
_Avoid_: Skill drill, drill (reserved for the MCQ engine), station, module, game

**Homepage**:
Joshua-facing shorthand for an individual Activity's intro/start page: the page that shows the Activity title, item count, overview copy, example, and Start action before the student begins the first Question Set.
_Avoid_: LSAT U landing page, Foyer, Activity Centre list

**Question Set**:
Codex-facing term for one complete per-question unit inside an Activity: the Question Type, the prompt, the response area, the coach feedback, the credited answer, and the answer key material used to judge the response. In the UI, this should still be labeled "Question N"; when Joshua says "question set," he means this full unit, not the collection of all questions.
_Avoid_: Question collection, drill set, item bank

# Classroom

The student's own room: the first surface a 1:1 student lands on. It is a reading surface, not a workspace. Its language is second person, and the coach is never named in the furniture.

## Terms

**Classroom frame** — the persistent layout every Classroom route is drawn in: the cream left rail, an optional middle list, and the white reading surface. Implemented by `ClassroomLayout`. Navigating between surfaces it draws swaps the content only and never remounts the rail. A page "sits in the Classroom frame" regardless of its URL; `/flagged` and `/journal` both do.

**StudentShell** — the older, separate frame with its own 240px rail, still drawing `/mainhub`, `/practice` and the rest. A student moving between a Classroom-frame page and a StudentShell page sees the whole page rebuild. Reducing that boundary is ongoing work, one surface at a time.

**Reading column** — the white content column. Most routes use one reading column on the right. A Wrong answers detail uses a four-column Classroom frame: cream rail, entries list, vertical question, and review panel. Below its journal-only breakpoints the entries list becomes an overlay and the review panel moves under the question.

**Drill landing workspace** — the pre-question surface at `/classroom/drill`, drawn inside the Classroom frame. It includes drill selection, launch controls, practice insights, and today's work; it excludes the official question-answering experience.
_Avoid_: Drill page (when the question experience could be confused with the landing surface), Mainhub
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

Saved. You're asking for fit on every screen size, not just one target size. That's the right goal, but one fact shapes the plan: past a certain point it becomes physically impossible. A phone turned sideways gives the page roughly 800×360, and Home's six must-see sections can't fit in that at readable sizes. So "never breaks" has to mean three things:
- Pages adjust continuously across a defined range.
- Below that range, they fall back in a set order.
- An automated check proves both on every change.

These questions pin that down.

**Q7.1 - Which devices count?** Earlier we left phones out.
- (a) Laptops and desktops only
- (b) Add tablets (iPad, portrait and landscape)
- (c) Everything, including phones

Recommended: (b) now, (c) as its own project later. Students do timed LR on laptops. Phones need a different layout, not just a smaller one.

---

**Q7.2 - Adjust continuously, or jump between fixed sizes?**
- (a) **Continuous**: text, spacing and card heights scale smoothly with the window's width and height, so every size in between gets its own exact fit.
- (b) **Fixed steps**: three or four preset layouts, switched at set widths. Sizes that fall between two steps can look slightly off.

Recommended: (a). It's the only way "fits any size" holds for screens nobody tested.

---

**Q7.3 - What gives first when a screen is too small?** On a tiny screen, something has to move below the fold. My proposed order, first to go at the top:
- **Home**: Next seven days, then the latest message, then the Smart Drill / Timed section buttons. Greeting, what's due and Assigned work always stay.
- **Drill page**: second Assigned work item, then Top priority, then Resume. The finder row, Smart Drill and Build the drill always stay.

Recommended: that order. Adjust it if any of those matter more to you.

---

**Q7.4 - How small can text go?** Shrinking text is the easiest way to fit, and the fastest way to make a page unreadable.

Recommended: body text never drops below 14px, headings never below 20px, and buttons stay large enough to click easily. Past those floors the page falls back per Q7.3 instead of shrinking further.

---

**Q7.5 - What happens on very large monitors?** On a 27" monitor at 2560 wide, stretching the content looks sparse and puts too much distance between elements.

Recommended: content grows up to a sensible maximum width and then stays centred. Text grows slightly, up to a cap.

---

**Q7.6 - How "never makes errors" is proven:**
- (a) I check sizes by hand and send screenshots.
- (b) An automated test opens Home, the Drill page and the Question screen at about 15 screen sizes, from a small laptop up to a 27" monitor. It fails the build if anything overlaps, gets cut off sideways, or pushes a must-see item below the fold. It runs before every release.

Recommended: (b), plus the screenshots for your eye. The test catches regressions from future changes; the screenshots catch "technically fits but looks wrong".

I've saved Round 7 to [the plan file](brainstorms/2026-09-24-lsatu-fit-one-screen.md). The questions are answered. The only open item is whether this matches what you have in mind.

**How it will work**
- **Screens:** laptops and desktops only.
- **Scaling:** text, spacing and card heights adjust smoothly with the window's width and height. There are no fixed jumps that break that. Your screen size (1536×695) is the main size I measure against, not the only one.
- **Small screens:** when something has to move below the fold, it goes in this order.
  - **Home:** Next seven days first, then the latest message, then the Smart Drill / Timed section buttons. The greeting, what's due and Assigned work always stay visible.
  - **Drill page:** the second Assigned work item first, then Top priority, then Resume. The finder row, Smart Drill and Build the drill always stay visible.
- **Big monitors:** content grows to a maximum width and stays centred. Text grows a little, up to a cap. The layout uses the extra room well instead of stretching.
- **Text size limits:** I start with body text no smaller than 14px and headings no smaller than 20px, and adjust those based on what actually looks right in the browser.
- **How I check it:** while I work, I look at Home and the Drill page in the Claude Code browser at your size, a small laptop, a big monitor and a narrow window. You get before-and-after screenshots, and nothing goes live until you approve.

**One change to what we agreed earlier:** Next seven days still shows on Home's first screen at your size and anything bigger. On a smaller laptop, it's the first thing to move below the fold.

**One thing I need from you before I start:** I'll run the app on your computer (a local test copy), and you'll need to sign in to it once in the Claude Code browser. To check Home the way a student with homework sees it, I'd also like to use a test student account. Tell me which account, or I'll pick one of your current students who has assigned work and view it read-only.

Is this the shared understanding? If yes, I'll start with the Drill page.

.drill-observatory-root {
  color: var(--observatory-ink);
  font-family: "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-canvas {
  isolation: isolate;
}

/* ── Shared micro-typography ─────────────────────────────────────────────── */

/* Tailwind's `font-mono` stack does not include the label face, so anything
   using it landed on ui-monospace — a different face from the labels beside it.
   The Today panel's date sat directly next to "TODAY" in two different monos. */
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-tools {
  align-items: center;
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 12px;
  justify-content: space-between;
  margin-bottom: -4px;
}

/* The address row takes the space that is left; the search trigger and the
   switch keep their intrinsic width and stay together on one line, wrapping as
   a pair rather than one above the other. */
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-tools > .qj {
  flex: 1 1 320px;
  width: auto;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-tools-aside {
  align-items: center;
  display: flex;
  flex: 0 0 auto;
  gap: 12px;
  margin-left: auto;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-tools-aside .drill-observatory-section-toggle {
  margin-left: 0;
}
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-deck {
  background: none;
  border: 0;
  border-radius: 0;
  box-shadow: none;
  gap: var(--observatory-space-5);
  overflow: visible;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice-bay,
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-today-bay {
  min-width: 0;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice,
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-launcher {
  background: var(--observatory-surface-card);
  border: 0;
  border-radius: var(--observatory-radius-shell);
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 1px var(--observatory-hairline),
    var(--observatory-shadow-card);
  height: auto;
  padding: var(--observatory-space-5) 22px;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice {
  gap: var(--observatory-space-6);
}

/* Tools are a hairline split, not two boxed cards. */
/* One column per tool. This was 1fr 1fr and stayed that way when Reading
   Comprehension made it three, which wrapped the third tile onto its own row
   under a hole. auto-fit keeps it honest as tools are added or removed. */
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice-tools {
  border: 0;
  display: grid;
  grid-auto-flow: column;
  grid-auto-columns: 1fr;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice-tile {
  background: transparent;
  border: 0;
  border-radius: var(--observatory-radius-control);
  gap: 10px;
  padding: 2px 24px 2px 2px;
  transition: background-color var(--observatory-motion), opacity var(--observatory-motion),
    transform var(--observatory-motion);
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-insight {
  align-items: end;
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 14px 20px;
  justify-content: space-between;
  padding: 18px 0;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-insight:first-child {
  padding-top: 0;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-insight:last-child {
  padding-bottom: 0;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-insight + .drill-observatory-insight {
  border-top: 1px solid var(--observatory-ring-quiet);
}

/* Analytics and Journal are records, not tools; they sit under their own
   hairline at the foot of the card. */
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-today {
  background: none;
  border-radius: 0;
  box-shadow: none;
  height: auto;
  padding: 0;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-today-inner {
  background: var(--observatory-surface-card);
  border-radius: var(--observatory-radius-shell);
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 1px var(--observatory-ring),
    var(--observatory-shadow-card);
  gap: var(--observatory-space-3);
  height: auto;
  overflow: hidden;
  padding: var(--observatory-space-5);
  position: relative;
}

/* ── Responsive ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */

/* The console is a fixed 392px by design. Below ~1080px of canvas the hero's
   left column starts losing the greeting, so the console narrows before the
   layout stacks. */
@media (max-width: 1199px) {
  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero-grid {
    gap: var(--observatory-space-5);
    grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) 356px;
    padding: var(--observatory-space-4) var(--observatory-space-5) var(--observatory-space-5);
  }

}

@media (max-width: 1023px) {
  /* The menu button lives in the sticky bar above this stage now, so the top
     inset is the hub's own spacing rather than clearance for a floating
     button. */
  .classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-stage {
    padding: var(--observatory-space-4) var(--observatory-space-5)
      var(--observatory-space-5);
  }

}

@media (max-width: 899px) {
  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero-grid {
    grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr);
    grid-template-rows: auto auto;
    min-height: 0;
    padding-top: var(--observatory-space-5);
  }

  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero {
    background-position: center 78%;
  }

  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-greeting,
  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-smart {
    align-self: start;
    max-width: none;
  }

  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-console {
    align-self: stretch;
    max-width: 460px;
  }
}

@media (max-width: 759px) {
  .classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-stage {
    padding: var(--observatory-space-4) var(--observatory-space-4)
      var(--observatory-space-5);
  }

  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero-grid {
    gap: var(--observatory-space-5);
    padding: var(--observatory-space-4);
  }

  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-console {
    max-width: none;
  }

  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice,
  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-launcher,
  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-today-inner,
  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-analytics {
    padding: var(--observatory-space-5);
  }

  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice-tools {
    grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr);
  }

  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice-tile {
    padding: 2px 2px 14px;
  }
[/external_agent_tool_result]

p='src/components/classroom/classroom.css'
s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
block='''/* ── Fit to the screen ─────────────────────────────────────────────────── */

/* The Drill page sizes itself from the window, continuously, so a laptop at
   Windows' default 125% scaling (1536x695 of page) shows the finder, both hero
   actions, Resume, Top priority and two assigned items without scrolling, and
   a large monitor gets a wider, roomier page instead of a small island.
   Every value is a clamp: a floor for small laptops, a cap for big monitors,
   and the window's height or width in between. No step changes. */
.drill-observatory-root {
  --fit-gap: clamp(12px, 2.4vh, 24px);
  --fit-pad: clamp(14px, 2.6vh, 28px);
  --fit-hero-min: clamp(160px, 25vh, 300px);
  --fit-title: clamp(1.35rem, min(3cqi, 4.4vh), 2.5rem);
  --fit-console: clamp(340px, 30cqi, 460px);
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-canvas {
  gap: var(--fit-gap);
  max-width: clamp(71.25rem, 82cqi, 96rem);
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-deck {
  gap: var(--fit-gap);
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero-grid {
  grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) var(--fit-console);
  min-height: var(--fit-hero-min);
  padding: calc(var(--fit-pad) * 0.8) var(--fit-pad);
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-smart h1,
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-console h2 {
  font-size: var(--fit-title);
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-console {
  gap: calc(var(--fit-gap) * 0.6);
  padding: var(--fit-pad);
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice,
.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-today-inner {
  padding: var(--fit-pad) clamp(18px, 1.6cqi, 28px);
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice {
  gap: var(--fit-gap);
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-insight {
  padding: clamp(10px, 2vh, 18px) 0;
}

.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-resume-row {
  min-height: clamp(50px, 7.5vh, 62px);
  padding: clamp(8px, 1.4vh, 12px) 14px;
}

/* ── Responsive ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */'''
a='/* ── Responsive ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */'
assert s.count(a)==1
s=s.replace(a,block)
# the fixed-width column override at 1199 would undo the fluid console
s=s.replace('''    gap: var(--observatory-space-5);
    grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) 356px;
    padding: var(--observatory-space-4) var(--observatory-space-5) var(--observatory-space-5);''','''    gap: var(--observatory-space-5);''')
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
p='src/pages/AcademyMainhub.tsx'
s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
s=s.replace('text-[clamp(1.35rem,3cqi,2rem)] font-semibold','font-semibold')
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
EOF

import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { toast } from "sonner";
import { useClassroomLayout } from "@/components/classroom/shell/ClassroomLayout";
import { useClassroomHome } from "./useClassroomHome";
import ClassroomHomeView from "./ClassroomHomeView";
import "./home.css";

/**
 * Classroom Home. Reads the student's own record and renders the dashboard;
 * every route it opens already exists in the rail, so this page is a shortcut
 * into the classroom rather than a second navigation.
 */
export default function ClassroomHome() {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const { openSection } = useClassroomLayout();
  const { model, loading, markDone, clearMessage, completingKey } = useClassroomHome();

  return (
    <ClassroomHomeView
      model={model}
      loading={loading}
      onOpen={(route) => navigate(route)}
      onSeeAllWork={() => openSection("work")}
      onMarkDone={markDone}
      onClearMessage={async (conversationId) => {
        try {
          await clearMessage(conversationId);
        } catch {
          toast.error("Could not clear that message. Try again.");
        }
      }}
      completingKey={completingKey}
    />
  );
}

html.dark .classroom-root.classroom-root-home .classroom-stage {
  background: linear-gradient(180deg, #131219, #0f0e17);
}

.classroom-root.classroom-root-home .classroom-column {
  max-width: 1260px;
  padding: 30px 44px 56px;
}

@media (max-width: 720px) {
  .classroom-root.classroom-root-home .classroom-column {
    padding: 24px 18px 48px;
  }
}

.classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-stage {
  background: var(--classroom-drill-page);
  padding: 8px 8px 8px 0;
}

.classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-column {
  max-width: none;
  padding: 8px;
}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

Writing the Home fit block.

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
command: cat >> src/components/classroom/home/home.css <<'EOF'

/* ── Fit to the screen ─────────────────────────────────────────────────── */

/* Home sizes itself from the window so a student with homework sees the
   greeting, what's due, Continue, the quick starts, Assigned work, the latest
   message and the next seven days on one screen at 1536x695 (Windows' default
   125% on a 1080p laptop). Every value is a clamp: a floor for small laptops,
   a cap for big monitors, the window's height in between. No step changes.
   On a shorter window the page scrolls from the bottom up, so Next seven days
   is the first thing to leave the screen and the greeting is the last. */
.classroom-root.classroom-root-home .classroom-column {
  max-width: clamp(1260px, 74vw, 1560px);
  padding: clamp(14px, 3vh, 30px) clamp(24px, 3vw, 44px) clamp(24px, 5vh, 56px);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home {
  gap: clamp(10px, 2vh, 22px);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-top {
  padding: clamp(18px, 3.2vh, 36px) clamp(22px, 2.6vw, 40px);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-title {
  font-size: clamp(32px, min(5cqi, 6.2vh), 60px);
  margin-top: clamp(6px, 1.4vh, 14px);
  margin-bottom: clamp(6px, 1.2vh, 12px);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-top-tools {
  gap: clamp(8px, 1.4vh, 12px);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-action-continue {
  min-height: clamp(64px, 11vh, 112px);
  padding: clamp(10px, 1.8vh, 18px) 20px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-main-card {
  padding: clamp(14px, 2.6vh, 26px) clamp(18px, 1.8vw, 26px);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-message-card {
  gap: clamp(8px, 1.4vh, 12px);
  padding: clamp(10px, 1.8vh, 18px) 22px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-message {
  padding: clamp(8px, 1.4vh, 14px) 14px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-week {
  margin-top: 0;
  padding-bottom: 0;
  gap: clamp(8px, 1.4vh, 12px);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-day {
  padding: clamp(8px, 1.4vh, 13px) 13px clamp(9px, 1.6vh, 15px);
}
EOF
[/external_agent_tool_call]

/* The name never breaks across lines ("Joshua / Fisseha"); if the greeting is
   too long, the whole name moves to the next line together. */
.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-title em {
  white-space: nowrap;
}

/* The greeting and its tools stayed side by side only above 900px of column,
   which a 1280px laptop with the rail open (about 880px) never reached, so the
   card stacked and doubled in height. Side by side holds down to 760px now. */

  .classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-masthead {
    grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1.05fr) minmax(300px, 0.95fr);

    width: auto;
  }
}

}

## assistant

The Drill page and Home now fit the screen at every laptop and desktop size I checked. Nothing is live on lsatprep.study; it's all on the [#135](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/135) branch waiting for your approval.

I didn't send the before-and-after screenshots I promised, because this browser pane has no way to save images. The local copy is open in the pane on the right so you can look for yourself. Resize the pane, or use its Viewport menu, to try different sizes.

**Drill page**
- The finder row, Smart Drill, Build the drill, Resume, Top priority and two assigned items fit without scrolling at every laptop size I tried:
  - 1536×695 (your laptop)
  - 1440×790 (a MacBook)
  - 1366×625 and 1280×600 (smaller laptops)
  - 1000×700 (a narrow window, with the sidebar folded into ☰)
- On a 1920 monitor the page now widens instead of sitting in a narrow strip.
- **Bug fixed:** Assigned work rows were running off the right edge of their card, which hid the "New" and "In progress" labels. That was happening on every screen size.

**Home**
- At 1536×695, a student with homework sees all six sections without scrolling: the greeting, what's due, Continue, Smart Drill / Timed section, Assigned work, the latest message, and Next seven days.
- At 1280×600, Next seven days is the first thing below the fold, as we agreed.
- **Bugs fixed:**
  - On 1280-wide laptops the greeting card was stacking into one column and doubling in height.
  - Your name was breaking as "Joshua / Fisseha". A long name now moves to the next line as a whole.
- Sizes adjust smoothly with the window. There are no jumps between fixed sizes.

**Things to know**
- **Home as a student:** your account is an admin, and admin accounts don't show the Assigned work card. So I checked Home with the sample student ("Ada") from the app's tests, not a real student account. To see it, open `localhost:8094/classroom?fitPreview=1` in the pane. That preview switch only exists on my local copy and isn't in what I've committed.
- **A file I didn't change:** starting the local copy made a Lovable plugin regenerate `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` and strip most of it. It's not in my commits. I tried to restore it and the command was blocked. Please don't commit that file; `git checkout -- supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` in the `lsatu-fit` folder puts it back.
- **Tests:** there are no type errors, and the Home tests pass. The one Drill page test that fails is about the PrepTest dropdown, which this work doesn't touch. I haven't confirmed whether it also fails on the main branch.
- **The Question screen** still has only the earlier untested change. It's next, with its own short round of questions.
- **Unchanged:** Home's grey-beige Assigned work card with the indented item is how the app already looks. I didn't change it; tell me if you want it changed.

Once you've looked, tell me it's approved or what to adjust. After that, I publish it (a frontend publish, no Lovable credits) and start on the Question screen.

## assistant

The rearrangement is showing in your pane, using the tester's real data. It looks like this:
- The greeting card on top. All four buttons (Smart Drill, Timed section, Inbox, Book a session) fit on one row on a wide screen.
- Assigned work on the left, with the latest message beside it on the right.
- Next seven days underneath.

**How it fits at each size**

| Screen | Result |
|---|---|
| **1536×695** (your laptop) | All six sections fit. Next seven days ends at 672 of 695. On the live site today it ends at 1051. |
| **1920×950** (big monitor) | Fits with room to spare (ends at 720). |
| **1280×600** (small laptop) | Everything except Next seven days fits, which is the fallback order we agreed. |
| **1000×700** (narrow window, sidebar folded) | Same as the small laptop: only Next seven days is below the fold. "Book a session" drops to a second row. |

**Two things to know when you look**
- The pane goes back to its own size when my turn ends, so what you see depends on how wide your pane is. To see it at your full screen size, drag the pane wider or make it full screen.
- It's temporary and only in that tab. Reloading the page removes it, and no student sees any of it. The live site is unchanged.

One thing I noticed in the screenshot: the message card now shows "New assignment: Spot the Relationship…". That's an automatic message, and it repeats what's already in Assigned work right next to it. It's not a layout problem, and I haven't changed anything about it.

If you approve this layout, I'll build it properly into the branch, re-check it at all five sizes, and add it to [#135](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/135). Then it's ready to publish when you say so.

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
command: cat >> src/components/classroom/home/home.css <<'EOF'

/* Assigned work and the latest message share one row, approved 2026-09-25.
   Stacked, a student with an overdue item, a second assignment and a message
   ran to 872px at 1536x695 even after the spacing above; side by side it
   ends at 672. Either card alone takes the full row. Below a 760px column
   everything stacks again. */
.classroom-root .classroom-home {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1.45fr) minmax(0, 1fr);
  align-items: stretch;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home > * {
  grid-column: 1 / -1;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home > .classroom-home-main-card:has(~ .classroom-home-message-card) {
  grid-column: 1;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home > .classroom-home-main-card ~ .classroom-home-message-card {
  grid-column: 2;
}

/* The assigned rows sat in a narrow island in the middle of the card. */
.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-main-card .classroom-home-main-content {
  flex: 1 1 auto;
  width: 100%;
}

/* The greeting's tools column gets the wider share so the four quick starts
   (Smart Drill, Timed section, Inbox, Book a session) sit on one row instead
   of leaving Book a session alone on a second one. */
.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-masthead {
  grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 0.9fr) minmax(360px, 1.1fr);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-actions {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(118px, 1fr));
  gap: 8px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-home .classroom-home-actions .classroom-home-action {
  justify-content: center;
  padding: 0 10px;
  white-space: nowrap;
}

@container classroom-home (max-width: 759.98px) {
  .classroom-root .classroom-home {
    grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr);
  }

  .classroom-root .classroom-home > .classroom-home-main-card:has(~ .classroom-home-message-card),
  .classroom-root .classroom-home > .classroom-home-main-card ~ .classroom-home-message-card {
    grid-column: 1 / -1;
  }
}
EOF
grep -n "container-name\|container: classroom-home\|container-type" src/components/classroom/home/home.css | head -3
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## assistant

The new Home layout is built into the branch and pushed to [#135](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/135). Nothing is live yet.

**What changed on Home**
- Assigned work and the latest message now sit side by side.
- The four buttons (Smart Drill, Timed section, Inbox, Book a session) sit on one row.
- The assigned items use the card's full width instead of floating in the middle.

**Checked on your tester account:** these are the tester's real items (overdue section, a second assignment, a message, the 47-day countdown), shown on the live site with the new styles applied only in that browser tab.

| Screen | Result |
|---|---|
| 1536×695 (your laptop) | Everything fits. Next seven days ends at 672. Today on the live site it ends at 1051. |
| 1920×950 (big monitor) | Fits with room to spare. |
| 1280×600 and 1000×700 (smaller laptop, narrow window) | Only Next seven days falls below the fold, the order we agreed. |
| 800 wide | Everything stacks into one column. |
| Only one of the two cards (no homework, or no message) | That card takes the full width. |

The Home tests pass.

**Two things I couldn't do**
- **I couldn't recheck this final version on my local copy.** You stopped the local server while I was working. I made one late fix to how the narrow-window stacking rule is written, and tested that version on the tester's live tab instead, not on my local copy.
- **The Drill page wasn't rechecked on the tester's real data** after the Home change. Its earlier checks at all sizes stand.

**Leftovers on your computer, none of them committed**

- The regenerated `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` from earlier is still sitting there changed. Don't commit it.
- The tester tab in the pane still has my preview styles. Reload it to see the real live site again.

**Next:** when you're happy with Home and the Drill page, say "publish" and I'll push them live. Publishing the front end costs no Lovable credits. After that, the Question screen gets its own short round of questions.

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
---
name: lovable-frontend-publish-is-separate
description: A push builds a Lovable preview but never reaches lsatprep.study; publish with deploy_project, wait for the sync, and prove it from the bundle.
metadata:
  type: project
---

Pushing to `main` makes Lovable build the commit — its project record shows a
preview screenshot tagged with the commit sha within minutes — but the published
site at lsatprep.study keeps serving the previous bundle until something calls
`deploy_project` (Lovable MCP, `project_id 53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`,
name `lsatu`). Confirmed 2026-09-08: a fix sat built-but-unpublished for 20
minutes with no error anywhere.

**The sync lags the push.** `deploy_project` publishes whatever Lovable has
ingested, not what is on origin. On 2026-09-09 a deploy fired seconds after a
successful push and shipped the *previous* commit; `get_project` showed
`latest_commit_sha` catching up about three minutes later, and a second deploy
was needed. There is no error and no warning — the deploy reports `pending` and
succeeds, just on the wrong commit.

**Why:** build, sync and publish are three separate steps, and none of them
reports that the others have not happened. A green push reads as shipped.

**How to apply:** before calling `deploy_project`, call `get_project` and check
`latest_commit_sha` equals your HEAD; if it does not, wait and re-check. After
deploying, prove it from the bundle: pick a string **the commit alone**
introduces — not a class name an earlier deploy already shipped, which is how a
stale bundle passed a check on 2026-09-09 — then fetch `https://lsatprep.study/`,
read the `index-*` asset hash out of it, and grep the asset for the marker.
Allow several minutes; the hash changing is the signal a publish landed at all.
Note `deploy_project` ships every unpublished commit on main, not just yours.

Same trap, different half: [[lovable-edge-functions-do-not-deploy-from-push]].
Related: [[push-only-your-own-commits]].
[/external_agent_tool_result]

# Session Handoff — LSAT U: make Drill page and Home fit one laptop screen (shipped); Question screen next

## Where it started
Joshua reported that LSAT U pages don't fit the screen at "100% zoom". Answer choices on the question screen are cut off, and the Drill page and Home need scrolling. Measured in his Chrome: Chrome zoom is 100%, but Windows display scaling is 125% (devicePixelRatio 1.25), so a 1920×1080 laptop gives the page 1536×695 CSS px. A grilling round then widened the goal: every page should fit and adjust continuously on any laptop or desktop size.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **Names** (in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu-fit\src\components\classroom\CONTEXT.md`):
  - Home = `/classroom` ("home", "homepage", "dashboard").
  - Drill page = `/classroom/drill`, always said with "page". A plain "drill" means a set of questions.
  - Question screen = `/drill` split view. The RC version is the RC question screen.
  - The Activity Centre's "Homepage" was renamed to Activity start page (`src\features\activity-centre\CONTEXT.md`).
  - StudentShell is marked retired.
- **Target sizes:** 1536×695 is the reference. Also checked 1920×950, 1440×790, 1366×625, 1280×600, and 1000×700 (sidebar folds into a menu). Laptops and desktops only; phones and tablets are out of scope.
- **Continuous scaling:** clamp()-based sizing on viewport height and width, no step jumps. Text floors start at 14px body and 20px headings, tuned by visual loop.
- **Fallback order on short screens:**
  - Home: Next seven days goes first, then the message, then the quick starts. Greeting, what's due and Assigned work always stay.
  - Drill page: the second assigned item goes first, then Top priority, then Resume. The finder row, Smart Drill and Build the drill always stay.
- **Big monitors:** content grows to a max width and stays centred.
- **Home rearrangement:** Joshua approved it after a live preview on the tester account. Assigned work sits beside the latest message, the four quick starts sit on one row, and assigned rows use the full card width.
- **Shipped:** PR vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db#135 was merged (merge commit `e1207da`) and published via Lovable `deploy_project` (free; project `53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`). The live CSS bundle is `/assets/index-BETDQJRn.css` and contains the `fit-console` marker. The publish also included two bot commits that only touch `src/integrations/supabase/types.ts`.
- **Bugs fixed:**
  - Assigned work rows overflowed their card: an implicit grid column was sized to max-content. Fixed in `src\components\mainhub\AssignedWorkPanel.tsx`.
  - Home's greeting card stacked on 1280px laptops (container breakpoint 900 changed to 760).
  - A name split across lines.
- **Code:**
  - Drill page: fit block in `src\components\classroom\classroom.css`, before "Responsive".
  - Home: fit and rearrangement blocks at the end of `src\components\classroom\home\home.css`.
  - Question screen: first-pass changes in `src\components\drill\official\OfficialQuestionPane.tsx` (text `calc(fontScale * clamp(17px, 2.2vh, 19px))`, tighter choice padding). Now live.
- **Log:** a ship entry was appended to `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\wiki\log.md`.

## Key files for next session
- Plan file: `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\brainstorms\2026-09-24-lsatu-fit-one-screen.md`. Holds all decisions from Rounds 1–8 and the status line.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu-fit\src\components\drill\official\OfficialQuestionPane.tsx`: Question screen layout (DrillSplit 53/47).
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu-fit\src\components\drill\official\OfficialDrillFrame.tsx`: the frame. LR uses `min-h-screen` / `min-h-[700px]` and does not pass `fillViewport`, so the whole page scrolls. RC (`src\features\rc\pages\RcPlayer.tsx:651`) passes `fillViewport` and its columns scroll independently.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu-fit\src\components\classroom\home\home.css` and `...\src\components\classroom\classroom.css`: shipped fit blocks.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\.claude\launch.json`: added config `lsatu-fit-dev` (port 8094, `--prefix projects/lsatu-fit`).
- Memory files touched: none.

## Running state
- Background processes: none running. Shells `bloi4obfk` and `br1aspato` both exited.
- Dev servers / ports: none. Joshua stopped `lsatu-fit-dev` :8094 and `lsatu-dev` :8080. Each localhost port has its own sign-in, and :8094 was last signed in as Joshua (admin).

  - `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu-fit` on branch `fix/fit-viewport` (already merged). It has a `node_modules` junction to `..\lsatu\node_modules`.
  - Uncommitted in that worktree, never commit:
    - Temp preview hook in `src\components\classroom\home\ClassroomHome.tsx`: `?fitPreview=1` swaps in the fixture from the new untracked file `src\components\classroom\home\__fitPreview.ts`.

## Verified state as of 02:15
- **lsatprep.study serves the new CSS.** Checked by curl: bundle `index-BETDQJRn.css`, `grep -c fit-console` = 1.
- **Home, live, tester account, 1536×695:** top 17–301, Assigned work 313–556 beside the message 320–563, Next seven days 567–672. Measured in the browser pane after the publish.
- **Drill page, live, tester, 1536×695:** the page fits. The hero ends at 351, and assigned rows' right edge (1483) is inside the card (1502).
- **Question screen, live, tester, 1536×695:** does NOT fit. Choice text is 17px, choices run from 248 to 768, and the document is 931 tall against a 695 viewport. Measured on a Smart Drill question, which was left unanswered.

- `curl -s https://lsatprep.study/ | grep -oE '/assets/index-[A-Za-z0-9_-]+\.css'`: expect `index-BETDQJRn.css` or newer. Grepping that asset for `fit-console` should return ≥1.

- Deferred: the Question screen fit (LR and RC). It needs its own grilling round. Proposals already on the table, not yet confirmed:
  - Auto-fit text per question with a 15px floor.

  - Data: 3,965 LR questions in `public/data/PT###-S#-LR.json`. Median 1,041 chars, max 2,412 (PT114-S2-Q23).

- OPEN: should the temp preview hook (`ClassroomHome.tsx` edit plus `__fitPreview.ts`) be removed from the lsatu-fit worktree now? Asked at the end, not answered.
